4084		// JavaScript / TypeScript
4085		// Ruby / PHP
4086		// Docs and agent guidance
4087		// Repository / tool dotfile config
4088		// Build systems
4089		// Task runners
4090		// CI config (location-based .github/workflows is handled above)
4091	// importantFileFamilies are base-name prefixes (already lowercase) whose
4092	// variants are all considered important. See matchesFileFamily.
4093	// Package provider holds the built-in provider templates: where each vendor
4094	// lives, which wire protocol it speaks, and which model to use by default.
4095	// Templates are data, mirroring crates/provider/providers.json in the Rust
4096	// implementation, but hard-coded so the Go binary needs no asset files. The
4097	// table only describes endpoints; credentials are read from the environment by
4098	// the caller via Template.APIKeyEnv / Template.APIKeyEnvs and the Auth helper.
4099	// Protocol names a wire protocol implemented by internal/llm.
4100	// Supported protocols.
4101		// ProtocolAnthropic is the Anthropic Messages API (llm.AnthropicClient).
4102		// ProtocolOpenAI is OpenAI chat completions (llm.Client).
4103		// ProtocolResponses is the OpenAI Responses API (llm.ResponsesClient).
4104	// Template describes one provider endpoint.
4105		// Name is the canonical id used by Resolve and on the command line.
4106		// Protocol is the wire protocol to use.
4107		// BaseURL is the API root; llm clients append their own path.
4108		// APIKeyEnv names the legacy single environment variable holding the key.
4109		// Empty means the endpoint is normally unauthenticated (local servers).
4110		// APIKeyEnvs names additional credential sources in priority order: the
4111		// first non-empty value wins. Auth tries APIKeyEnv first for compatibility,
4112		// then these names.
4113		// NoAuth skips credential lookup entirely and sends no auth header.
4114		// It models apiKey:false in the upstream provider options.
4115		// DefaultModel is used when the user does not name a model.
4116		// CacheSupport reports that the provider supports prompt caching on at
4117		// least one protocol. It does not force wire markers: llm still emits them
4118		// only on protocols with an explicit stable-prefix representation, and
4119		// local/open-compatible endpoints stay cache-free by default.
4120	// templates is the built-in table, ordered as it should be presented to users.
4121	// aliases maps alternate spellings onto canonical names.
4122	// List returns the built-in templates in presentation order. The slice is a
4123	// copy; mutating it does not affect the table.
4124	// Resolve looks up a template by canonical name or alias, case-insensitively.
4125	// The second result reports whether the name was known.
4126	// NormalizeModel canonicalizes a model id across templates: surrounding
4127	// whitespace is trimmed, unicode dashes/underscores become '-', repeated
4128	// separators collapse, and a case variant of a built-in default resolves back
4129	// to that default's canonical spelling. Unknown ids keep their own casing,
4130	// including vendor prefixes such as "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5".
4131	// Model returns the normalized override, or the template's canonical default
4132	// when override is empty. Callers in app/cli can use this hook without the
4133	// provider package knowing about config or profiles.
4134			// short aliases and normalisation
4135			// unknown / empty fall back to none
4136			// unknown effort falls back to an empty override
4137		// An effort protocol ignores the budget and a budget protocol ignores the
4138		// effort string: neither invents a knob the other side does not understand.
4139		// A toggle protocol reports "on" for any recognized effort or a positive
4140		// budget, and "off" for an empty request.
4141		// Unknown protocols return the zero config regardless of the request.
4142		// Forcing this at the data layer: the zero Options must leave Thinking nil,
4143		// so no default request carries a thinking field until wiring lands.
4144	// Thinking support kinds returned by ThinkingSupport. They describe how a wire
4145	// protocol expresses a reasoning control, not whether it is enabled.
4146	// ThinkingSupport classifies a wire protocol by the shape of its reasoning
4147	//   - "effort": OpenAI chat-completions and Responses use a named level
4148	//     ("low"/"medium"/"high") in reasoning_effort / reasoning.effort.
4149	//   - "budget": Anthropic Messages uses an explicit budget_tokens value on its
4150	//     extended-thinking block.
4151	//   - "toggle": a boolean on/off switch. No built-in protocol uses it today;
4152	//     it is reserved for providers that expose only a toggle, and lets
4153	//     ResolveThinking still normalize a request for such a protocol.
4154	//   - "none": the protocol has no reasoning control. This is also the fallback
4155	//     for an empty or unrecognised name.
4156	// Matching ignores surrounding whitespace and case. Both the canonical wire
4157	// names from internal/llm and the common short provider aliases are accepted.
4158	// The four support kinds themselves are also accepted and returned unchanged,
4159	// so the function is idempotent and callers can pass an already-classified
4160	// kind. This is a pure function: no I/O and no global state.
4161	// Thinking budget bounds. Anthropic's extended thinking rejects budgets below
4162	// 1024 tokens; the upper bound keeps an unreasonable request from being
4163	// forwarded while sitting well above any current model's useful allowance.
4164	// normalizeEffort maps a user-supplied reasoning effort to the canonical
4165	// lowercase level understood by the OpenAI-style wire fields. Surrounding
4166	// whitespace is trimmed and matching is case-insensitive; common CJK spellings
4167	// unrecognised or empty value returns "" so no invalid level is ever forwarded.
4168	// clampBudget bounds a thinking-token budget. A non-positive value means
4169	// "unset" and stays 0 so the caller emits no budget field at all; a positive
4170	// value is clamped into [minThinkingBudget, maxThinkingBudget].
4171	// ResolveThinking normalizes a user's thinking request for a wire protocol. It
4172	// is a pure function: no I/O, no mutation of inputs, and no dependency on
4173	// config or session state. The returned llm.ThinkingConfig is data for a
4174	// future wire-client wiring step; until that step lands, leaving
4175	// llm.Options.Thinking nil keeps the current wire bytes unchanged.
4176	//   - effort protocols keep only a normalized Effort level;
4177	//   - budget protocols keep only a clamped BudgetTokens value;
4178	//   - toggle protocols set Enabled only when the request asks for "on";
4179	//   - unsupported protocols return the zero ThinkingConfig.
4180	// diagnosticsFields returns the set of Field values carrying a given severity.
4181		// Every built-in template must also stand on its own, independent of the
4182		// alias cross-checks performed by ValidateAll.
4183		// Validating a single template cannot see cross-template duplicates.
4184	// Package provider template validation.
4185	// This file turns the structural assumptions that were previously spread
4186	// across tests (non-empty fields, parseable endpoint URL, known protocol) into
4187	// one reusable, side-effect-free diagnostic API. A CLI `doctor` path or a
4188	// config loader can call Validate/ValidateCatalog/ValidateAll and surface the
4189	// findings without the provider package importing config or the network.
4190	// Nothing here performs I/O or consults the environment: validation only looks
4191	// at the shape of a Template. Findings are returned, never logged or panicked.
4192	// Severity classifies a validation finding.
4193	// Supported severities. Errors make a template unusable; warnings describe a
4194	// suspicious but potentially intentional configuration.
4195	// Diagnostic is one finding about a template. Template is the canonical name
4196	// under which the finding was produced (empty when the name itself is broken),
4197	// Field is the Template field (or "catalog" for cross-template findings), and
4198	// Message is a human-readable explanation.
4199	// String renders a diagnostic for direct display: "[severity] template/field:
4200	// message". Empty Template/Field components are omitted.
4201	// HasErrors reports whether any finding is an error (as opposed to a warning).
4202	// Validate checks one template for structural problems. It returns findings in
4203	// a stable order (Name, Protocol, BaseURL, DefaultModel, credentials, protocol
4204	// consistency), never mutates t, and returns an empty slice for a sound
4205	// template. An empty slice is always non-nil so callers can append freely.
4206	// validateBaseURL reports malformed endpoint URLs. Only absolute http(s) URLs
4207	// usable by the llm clients are accepted; credentials, query strings and
4208	// fragments in a base URL are almost always a copy/paste mistake.
4209	// validateCredentials checks the shape and mutual consistency of credential
4210	// sources. A missing key on a loopback endpoint is fine; the same omission on
4211	// a remote endpoint is suspicious enough to warn about.
4212	// validateProtocolURL applies a deliberately conservative consistency rule:
4213	// the last base-URL path segment should match the protocol's usual API root.
4214	// It warns rather than errors because vendors sometimes expose a nonstandard
4215	// path; a mismatch usually means the wrong protocol was chosen for the target.
4216	// lastPathSegment returns the final non-empty segment of a URL path, ignoring
4217	// surrounding slashes. An empty path yields an empty string.
4218	// ValidateCatalog checks a set of templates and adds cross-template findings
4219	// that Validate alone cannot see, currently duplicate canonical names. The
4220	// input slice is not modified and findings follow input order.
4221	// ValidateAll validates the built-in template table together with its alias
4222	// map: every template, unique names, and aliases that resolve to a real
4223	// canonical name without shadowing one. Alias findings use Field "aliases" and
4224	// a stable (sorted) order so output is reproducible.
4225	// assertStoredWatched checks the registry's own copy: that is the one
4226	// refreshLocked iterates, so the flag must be set there (not just on the value
4227	// returned to the caller).
4228	// TestStartAndPromoteMarkJobsWatched guards the fix at its source: both paths
4229	// that spawn an in-process completion watcher must flag the stored job so
4230	// refreshLocked leaves its exit code to that watcher.
4231	// TestWatchedJobNeverShowsPrematureTerminalExit reproduces the race window
4232	// deterministically: the child is already dead (unrecoverable pid) but the
4233	// reaper has not yet recorded the real exit code. Readers must not publish a
4234	// terminal Exited/-1 snapshot during that window — only the reaper may make the
4235	// job terminal, and it carries the real code.
4236		// Get must not turn the dead pid into a terminal -1 snapshot.
4237		// Neither may List.
4238		// Once the reaper records the real code, the terminal snapshot is valid
4239		// and carries that exact code, never the -1 sentinel.
4240	// TestWatchedFlagIsNotPersisted keeps the on-disk JSON shape unchanged and
4241	// verifies the intended consequence: a registry reopened from disk has no live
4242	// watcher, so it must not inherit the marker and must still refresh dead pids.
4243	// startHelperChild spawns the sleep-helper test binary as a real child outside
4244	// the registry. It stands for the innocent process that may inherit a stale
4245	// job's pid after a crash, so the tests can prove it is never signalled.
4246		// Reap in the background: kill(pid, 0) still succeeds for a zombie on
4247		// Unix, so an unreaped child would keep looking alive after Stop.
4248	// TestLoadRetiresAJobWhosePidWasRecycled checks the state right after reopening
4249	// the index, before any Get/List refresh can run: load itself must not leave a
4250	// recycled pid marked as running.
4251	// TestRefreshRetiresAJobWhosePidWasRecycled injects the stale job directly, so
4252	// refreshLocked (not load) is the code under test.
4253		// The stale job is kept but retired, so a retry cannot kill anything.
4254	// TestStopKillsAReloadedJobWithMatchingIdentity proves the check does not
4255	// disable legitimate stops: only the raw-pid path of a reloaded job is used
4256	// here, and its recorded start time matches the real child.
4257	// TestStartInSessionScopesJobsAndFiltersThem covers the isolation guarantee:
4258	// jobs of two sessions sharing one registry are independent, ListForSession
4259	// filters by session, and plain Start keeps its legacy global sequence.
4260		// The session is recorded on the job; a plain Start still has none.
4261		// Ids are namespaced by session, never collide, and starting session jobs
4262		// did not consume the legacy counter: the plain job is still job1.
4263		// Filtering returns only the requested session's jobs.
4264		// The unchanged List still sees every job.
4265	// TestLegacyJobsWithoutSessionStayVisible locks in jobs.json compatibility: a
4266	// missing "session" key decodes to the empty session, so old jobs remain in the
4267	// global list and in ListForSession(""), and cannot collide with new ids.
4268		// No "session" and no "sessionNext" keys: exactly what an older build wrote.
4269		// New ids must not reuse the persisted legacy id or the loaded counter.
4270		// The legacy job is still visible alongside the new ones.
4271	// TestSessionIDsSurviveReopen verifies the session counter is persisted, so a
4272	// restarted registry never reuses an id whose log may still be on disk.
4273	// TestConcurrentSessionStartsGetUniqueIDs checks the session counter under the
4274	// race detector when several sessions share one registry concurrently.
4275	// TestSessionIDIsSafeAsAFileName ensures odd session names cannot turn an id
4276	// into a path that escapes the registry directory when it becomes a log name.
4277		// A finished job must be left alone: StopAll only touches running jobs.
4278		// The job that had already exited keeps its terminal state.
4279	// stubbornLive is a LiveProcess whose Kill deliberately leaves Done open, so
4280	// its watcher never finishes and StopAll has to fall back to the ctx deadline.
4281		// The job was still signalled before the wait began.
4282	// shellCommand builds a command line for the platform's shell.
4283	// longCommand runs this test binary in a dedicated sleep mode. Using ping(1)
4284	// here made the suite depend on an external tool and on pipe setup; on Windows
4285	// that occasionally failed with ERROR_NO_DATA while the runner was busy.
4286	// TestSleepHelperProcess is only meaningful when launched by longCommand.
4287			// Stop only signals the child; its reaper writes a final snapshot
4288			// afterwards. Wait for that watcher so TempDir's RemoveAll cannot race
4289			// with a .jobs-*.tmp file created after the directory was scanned.
4290		// Pruning keeps the log: it is the only trace of what the job did.
4291		// A fresh id must not collide with the persisted one.
4292		// A job written by a process that is no longer running: its exit code is
4293		// unrecoverable, so it must report -1 rather than pretend.
4294	// TestWaitIdleWaitsForBackgroundWatchers locks in the teardown guarantee the
4295	// flaky TestPruneForgetsFinishedJobsOnly cleanup relies on: a registry is only
4296	// quiescent once every completion watcher has finished its final index write.
4297	// Stop itself is asynchronous, so removing the directory before waitIdle can
4298	// race with a watcher that creates a .jobs-*.tmp file after RemoveAll scanned
4299	// the directory ("directory not empty" on CI).
4300		// The watcher is blocked on live.Done(); waitIdle must not return yet.
4301		// Releasing the watcher lets it finish, including its final persist.
4302	// detach puts the job in its own session so it survives the shell that started
4303	// it and can be killed as a group.
4304	// alive reports whether pid is a running process.
4305		// EPERM means the process exists but belongs to another user.
4306	// sameProcess reports whether pid is alive AND was created at startedAt. A pid
4307	// alone is not an identity: once a job is reloaded from jobs.json the OS may
4308	// have recycled the number for an unrelated process.
4309	// Linux exports the process start time as clock ticks since boot in
4310	// /proc/<pid>/stat (field 22, in USER_HZ = 100 ticks/s) and the boot-relative
4311	// wall clock in /proc/uptime. Other Unix systems have no /proc, so the check
4312	// fails closed there: an unverifiable job is treated as stale and never killed.
4313		// Derive the boot instant from the same "now" used to read uptime, then
4314		// add the process's own offset from boot. Both readings are within a few
4315		// milliseconds of each other; identityTolerance absorbs the skew.
4316	// processStartTicks reads field 22 (starttime) from /proc/<pid>/stat.
4317		// comm (field 2) is parenthesized and may contain spaces or ')', so the
4318		// last ')' ends it. The fields after it begin at state (field 3), making
4319		// starttime (field 22) index 19.
4320	// systemUptime reads the boot-relative wall clock from /proc/uptime.
4321	// killTree terminates pid and every descendant.
4322		// Setsid made the child a session leader, so -pid is its whole group.
4323	// TestStopAndRemoveLeaveWatchedExitCodeToWatcher pins down the division of
4324	// responsibility for a job's terminal state. For a watched job (one with an
4325	// in-process reaper/watchLive), the real exit code belongs to that watcher:
4326	// a dead or recycled pid must never be turned into a premature Exited/-1 by
4327	// Stop or Remove. The identity guard only refuses to signal an unidentifiable
4328	// pid; it must leave the job running for the watcher. For an unwatched job
4329	// (reloaded from disk, no watcher) there is nobody else to recover a code, so
4330	// the existing retire-with-(-1) behaviour is preserved.
4331				// The watcher still owns the terminal snapshot. Once it records the
4332				// real code, Get must report that code — never the -1 that a
4333				// premature retire here would have frozen in place.
4334	// detach gives the job its own process group and no console, so closing the
4335	// terminal that started it does not take it down.
4336	// alive reports whether pid is a running process.
4337	// sameProcess reports whether pid is alive AND was created at startedAt. A pid
4338	// alone is not an identity: once a job is reloaded from jobs.json the OS may
4339	// have recycled the number for an unrelated process. GetProcessTimes gives the
4340	// kernel's creation timestamp for that exact process object, so a recycled pid
4341	// with a different creation time is rejected.
4342	// killTree terminates pid and every descendant.
4343	// A detached shell's children are not in a job object here, so taskkill /T is
4344	// the only way to reach the whole tree.
4345	// Package jobs tracks background processes that outlive a turn.
4346	// A job is a detached child whose stdout and stderr go to a log file under the
4347	// registry directory. The registry itself is a JSON file, so the jobs of a
4348	// previous session are still listed after a restart: a live pid is reported as
4349	// running, a dead one as exited.
4350	// Status is how a job is doing.
4351		// StatusRunning means the process is still alive.
4352		// StatusExited means it finished on its own.
4353		// StatusKilled means Stop (or Remove) terminated it.
4354	// Constants that shape the registry.
4355		// DefaultTailLines is what Tail returns when asked for zero lines.
4356		// storeFile is the JSON index beside the logs.
4357		// tailWindow bounds how much of a log Tail reads.
4358		// identityTolerance is the clock slack allowed when comparing a persisted
4359		// StartedAt with a live process's kernel creation time. Both timestamps
4360		// come from the same machine; the slack only covers the few milliseconds
4361		// between the two readings (plus /proc's 10 ms tick granularity on Unix).
4362	// errProcessIdentity marks a refusal to signal a pid whose creation time does
4363	// not match the recorded job: after a crash the OS may have recycled that pid
4364	// for an unrelated process.
4365	// Job is one background process, as it is persisted.
4366		// Session optionally scopes the job to a session. The empty value keeps
4367		// jobs.json written by older builds readable: a missing "session" key
4368		// unmarshals to "", so those jobs remain global/unscoped and visible.
4369		// ExitCode is -1 while the job runs, and for a job whose exit code is no
4370		// longer recoverable (the process died while the registry was not watching).
4371		// live is set on promoted jobs so Stop can signal the exact child instead
4372		// of relying only on a pid that an injected fake may not really own.
4373		// watched marks jobs whose completion is tracked in this process by a
4374		// reaper (Start) or watcher (PromoteFromTimeout). refreshLocked must leave
4375		// these to that watcher: a child can die before the watcher records its
4376		// real exit code, and a pid-only refresh would otherwise publish a
4377		// terminal Exited/-1 snapshot in that window. Not persisted — a registry
4378		// reopened from disk has no watcher and must fall back to pid refresh.
4379	// Running reports whether the job is still going.
4380	// Terminal reports whether the job has finished.
4381	// Elapsed is how long ago the job started.
4382	// Command describes the process to start.
4383		// Program is the executable; it is not resolved through a shell.
4384		// Args are passed unchanged.
4385		// Dir is the working directory; empty keeps the registry's current one.
4386		// Env entries ("K=V") are appended to the inherited environment.
4387	// Registry owns the jobs of a session and their log files.
4388	// Every method is safe to call from several goroutines. Two locks keep the
4389	// in-memory state independent of disk I/O: mu guards jobs/next and is held only
4390	// long enough to mutate state and copy a snapshot; ioMu serializes the actual
4391	// JSON encoding and atomic replace. Readers never initiate a disk write — they
4392	// only mark the registry dirty, and the next write path or the reaper persists
4393	// the full snapshot. A version number discards snapshots that were overtaken
4394	// by a newer state change.
4395	// A reader may still wait for a write that is already in flight, so a status
4396	// it observes is also on disk by the time it returns. That wait releases mu
4397	// (sync.Cond.Wait), so it never blocks other readers or writers.
4398		// bgWork tracks completion watchers (the reaper spawned by Start and the
4399		// watcher spawned by PromoteFromTimeout). A watcher owns the final index
4400		// write for its job; waitIdle lets callers that are about to tear down the
4401		// registry directory make sure no background goroutine will write into it
4402		// afterwards. Add happens before the goroutine starts; tests call waitIdle
4403		// only after they are done starting jobs.
4404	// New opens (or creates) a registry whose logs live under dir.
4405	// Dir is where the index and the logs live.
4406	// waitIdle blocks until every completion watcher started by this registry has
4407	// finished, including the final index write each one performs.
4408	// Stop and Remove only signal the child: they return before its reaper has
4409	// observed the exit and persisted the result. A caller that is about to delete
4410	// the registry directory must wait here as well, or a watcher can create a
4411	// .jobs-*.tmp file after the directory was scanned and break teardown with
4412	// "directory not empty". Call waitIdle only once no more jobs will be started.
4413	// Start launches cmd detached and returns the recorded job.
4414	// The job is unscoped (Session is empty) and gets a legacy global "job%d" id,
4415	// exactly as before session support was added. Use StartInSession to tag a job
4416	// with a session.
4417	// StartInSession is Start with a session scope. The session is stored on the
4418	// job and persisted with `omitempty`, so an empty session is identical to Start
4419	// and older jobs.json files (which have no "session" key) stay readable.
4420	// Session ids are namespaced (job<N>@<session>) and drawn from a counter
4421	// separate from the legacy one, so starting a session job never changes the id
4422	// a later plain Start would receive, and two sessions sharing one registry
4423	// directory can never collide.
4424		// The child holds its own copies now.
4425		// Keep the returned value and the registry's pointer in SEPARATE storage:
4426		// storing &job would make the caller's returned copy alias the same memory
4427		// that reap() mutates under mu, which is a data race on Job.Status/ExitCode.
4428		// Encoding and writing happen without mu held: concurrent readers and
4429		// other writers are never blocked by the disk.
4430	// reap records the real exit code as soon as the child dies.
4431		// Stop may already have marked the job killed; never overwrite that
4432		// terminal state with an exit that the reaper happened to observe.
4433	// List returns every job, oldest first, after refreshing dead processes.
4434		// A refresh only marks the registry dirty; no disk write happens here.
4435		// Let any in-flight write land before copying. Cond.Wait releases mu, so
4436		// this never blocks another reader or writer. Copying after the wait keeps
4437		// the returned snapshot consistent with the state that reached the disk.
4438	// ListForSession returns the jobs tagged with session, oldest first.
4439	// It filters the result of List, so a caller only ever sees (and can only act
4440	// on) its own jobs; jobs of other sessions sharing this registry directory are
4441	// hidden. An empty session selects the legacy, unscoped jobs — including any
4442	// loaded from a jobs.json written before the Session field existed. Use List
4443	// for an unfiltered view of every job.
4444	// Get returns one job by id.
4445		// A refresh only marks the registry dirty; no disk write happens here.
4446		// Let any in-flight write land before copying the snapshot (see List).
4447		// Cond.Wait released mu, so a watcher may have updated (or a concurrent
4448		// caller may have removed) this job. Re-read the map and copy only now, so
4449		// the returned value matches the settled, persisted state.
4450	// Stop kills a job and its descendants.
4451	// A job reloaded from disk has no live handle, so its only reference to the
4452	// child is a raw pid. Pids are recycled: after a crash the number may now
4453	// belong to an unrelated process. Such a job must be re-identified by its
4454	// creation time before it is signalled. A live handle is authoritative (it
4455	// refers to the very child we started), so that path needs no pid check.
4456			// A watched job's terminal state and real exit code belong to its
4457			// in-process reaper/watcher. A dead or recycled pid must not be
4458			// published as Exited/-1 here — that is the same premature-terminal
4459			// race refreshLocked already avoids for watched jobs. Leave the job
4460			// running and let the watcher record the true code; we only refuse to
4461			// signal an unidentifiable pid.
4462	// StopAll signals every running job and waits for its completion watcher to
4463	// reap it. Stop itself only signals the child; the reapers spawned by Start and
4464	// the watchers spawned by PromoteFromTimeout record the real exit later. Waiting
4465	// here means that when StopAll returns a nil error, no watched job is still
4466	// being reaped.
4467	// The wait is bounded by ctx: pass a context with a timeout to cap the total
4468	// time spent. Every job is signalled before the wait begins, so an expired ctx
4469	// never leaves a running job unsignalled — it only means a watcher did not
4470	// finish in time. The returned error joins each individual Stop failure with
4471	// ctx.Err() when the deadline fires.
4472	// StopAll is idempotent and safe to call concurrently with readers and with
4473	// other StopAll calls. It is meant for an explicit teardown: callers must not
4474	// start new jobs while it runs, since the wait covers every watcher started so
4475	// far. An empty (or already fully stopped) registry is a no-op.
4476		// Snapshot under mu: Stop mutates statuses and cannot be called under mu.
4477		// waitIdle has no timeout, so drive it on a goroutine and bound this call
4478		// by ctx. A registry whose jobs all came from disk has no watchers, so this
4479		// returns at once.
4480	// Tail returns the last n lines of a job's log.
4481	// Remove forgets a job and deletes its log.
4482	// As with Stop, a reloaded job's raw pid is re-identified before it is
4483	// signalled. On a mismatch nothing is killed: the job is retired instead and
4484	// the error asks the caller to remove it again once the stale state is settled.
4485			// As in Stop, a watched job's terminal state belongs to its in-process
4486			// watcher: retiring it with -1 here would publish a premature terminal
4487			// snapshot and hide the real exit code the watcher is about to record.
4488	// Prune forgets finished jobs that started more than age ago.
4489	// Their logs stay on disk: the log is the only trace of what a job did. An age
4490	// of zero forgets every finished job.
4491	// refreshLocked flips jobs whose process vanished into exited. It reports
4492	// whether anything changed. An exit code we can no longer recover stays -1.
4493	// A refresh is a state change like any other: it bumps the version and marks
4494	// the registry dirty. Read paths never write the index themselves — the next
4495	// write path or the reaper persists the full snapshot.
4496			// Jobs with an in-process watcher (Start reaper, promote watcher) own
4497			// their own exit code. A dead pid alone must not publish a terminal
4498			// Exited/-1 snapshot before that watcher has recorded the real code.
4499			// A live pid is not proof: it may have been recycled by the OS after
4500			// the registry was reloaded. Require the creation time to match too.
4501	// allocateID returns an id that is unique within the registry.
4502	// The empty session keeps the legacy global "job%d" sequence, so Start and
4503	// PromoteFromTimeout behave exactly as before. A named session uses a separate
4504	// counter and a "@session" suffix; the numeric part never overlaps the legacy
4505	// sequence, which keeps plain Start ids stable even after session jobs exist.
4506	// The collision loop still guards against reusing an id left behind by an
4507	// older index.
4508	// idToken reduces a session name to characters that are safe in a file name,
4509	// because an id is also the base name of the job's log file. The numeric prefix
4510	// guarantees uniqueness, so two sessions that map to the same token cannot
4511	// produce the same id.
4512	// index is the on-disk shape. SessionNext is additive: an index written before
4513	// session support has no such key and decodes to zero, which is fine because
4514	// no session-scoped ids existed yet.
4515			// Jobs read back from disk have no live watcher and no pid capability.
4516			// Retire any "running" entry whose pid is gone or now belongs to a
4517			// different process, so a reader can never mistake a recycled pid for
4518			// the original child. No disk write happens here; the corrected state
4519			// is picked up by the next write path.
4520	// markStale retires a job whose process is gone or can no longer be identified
4521	// with confidence. Its real exit code is unrecoverable, so it stays at -1.
4522	// noteChangeLocked records an in-memory mutation. Caller must hold mu.
4523	// changedLocked records a mutation and returns the snapshot to persist plus
4524	// the version it represents. Caller must hold mu and must call persist after
4525	// unlocking; no disk I/O happens while mu is held. It marks a write in flight
4526	// so readers can wait for it to reach the disk.
4527	// finishWrite balances a changedLocked call. It is deferred by persist so the
4528	// in-flight count is cleared on every return path, including errors.
4529	// snapshotLocked copies the registry state for persistence. Caller must hold mu.
4530	// persist atomically writes a versioned snapshot to disk. Encoding and file
4531	// I/O happen under ioMu but with mu released, so they never block readers or
4532	// other writers of in-memory state. Snapshots overtaken by a newer version are
4533	// skipped: the newer snapshot is written by its own caller.
4534		// A unique temporary name avoids clobbering a file another registry
4535		// instance may be writing into the same directory.

4537			// The window starts mid-line; drop that fragment.

4540		// The completion watcher must not overwrite the killed status.

4543	// LiveProcess is the running child a tool timeout hands to the registry. It is

4546		// Kill terminates the child and its descendants.
4547		// Done is closed once the child has been reaped.
4548		// ExitCode is valid after Done is closed.
4549	// PromoteFromTimeout records an already-running child as a background job.
4550	// logPath is the file the child is already writing to: it holds the output

4560	// hangGuard is a generous safety net so a regression cannot hang the whole

4591		// The next id must not collide with the persisted one.

4595	// TestStaleSnapshotDoesNotOverwriteNewerState guards the version check: an

4597	// written over the fresher index.
4598		// A snapshot captured before "b" existed, but written afterwards. Go
4599		// through changedLocked so the in-flight accounting stays balanced.
4600	// feedChunks drives the streaming wrapper with the given chunk boundaries and
4601	// returns everything it emits, flushing any tail at EOF.
4602		// Reusing the caller's read buffer must not corrupt the retained bytes.
4603	// stripTerminalQueries removes terminal query/report escape sequences from one
4604	// chunk of PTY output and returns the clean bytes plus any trailing partial
4605	// escape sequence that must be prepended to the next chunk before it is passed
4606	// here again.
4607	// Why this exists: CLIs such as gh/npm — and, worse, attacker-controlled tool
4608	// output — emit sequences that ask the terminal to report its own state:
4609	//   - DSR:              ESC [ 6 n  (and ESC [ 5 n)
4610	//   - OSC colour query: ESC ] 11 ; ? BEL  (or ESC ] 10 ; ? ST, ESC ] 4 ; n ; ? …)
4611	//   - DA / DA2:         ESC [ c    (and ESC [ > c)
4612	//   - DECRQSS:          ESC [ $ q
4613	// A real terminal answers each of these by injecting *more* escape sequences
4614	// into the byte stream. In argentgo that stream is rendered by the TUI/native
4615	// frontends and echoed into the model, so the answer becomes a prompt-injection
4616	// channel. Legitimate display sequences (SGR colour/bold, cursor movement,
4617	// charset designators, OSC hyperlinks/titles) are kept byte-for-byte.
4618	// The filter is byte-oriented and only ever holds back ASCII escape bytes, so a
4619	// UTF-8 rune split across chunk boundaries is emitted intact; keeping the
4620	// stream well-formed is the caller's UTF-8 carry writer's job, not this one's.
4621				// Copy rather than alias: callers reuse their Read buffer, so the
4622				// pending bytes must not point into chunk's backing array.
4623	// scanEscape parses the escape sequence beginning at chunk[i] (which must be
4624	// ESC). It returns the index just past the sequence, whether it is a terminal
4625	// query that should be dropped, and whether the sequence is incomplete in this
4626	// chunk and must be held back.
4627	// scanCSI parses a CSI whose body starts at body (just after ESC '[').
4628			// Consume up to (not including) this byte and let the caller
4629			// resynchronise on it, rather than swallowing unrelated text.
4630	// scanOSC parses an OSC whose body starts at body (just after ESC ']'). An OSC
4631	// is a query when its payload contains the ";?" pattern (OSC 10/11/12/4/52
4632	// queries). ST is ESC '\'; BEL also terminates OSC.
4633	// scanStringEscape parses a DCS/SOS/PM/APC body (after its introducer) up to a
4634	// string terminator. These are never queries, but they must still be tracked so
4635	// a terminator split across chunks is not torn apart.
4636	// queryFilter is a streaming wrapper around stripTerminalQueries: it keeps the
4637	// trailing partial sequence between Write calls, so callers can feed it chunks
4638	// from repeated Read calls without managing pending bytes themselves. It is not
4639	// safe for concurrent use — each PTY stream has a single reader goroutine.
4640	// Write filters one chunk and returns only bytes that are safe to emit now.
4641	// Flush emits any retained partial sequence at EOF. A dangling fragment cannot

4666		// TIOCSWINSZ makes the kernel deliver SIGWINCH to the foreground group.

4675	// TestWaitConcurrentCloseNeverErrors reproduces a ConPTY use-after-close.

4741			// Blocks until conhost is gone; after the child exits that is quick.

4758	// environmentBlock builds the double-NUL terminated UTF-16 block CreateProcessW

4783	// Supported reports whether this build can create a pseudoterminal.
4784	// Start launches cmd inside a new pseudoterminal of the given size.
4785	// Cancelling ctx kills the child and closes the terminal.
4786	// Read reads whatever the terminal produced next.
4787	// It returns io.EOF once the child exited and the stream is drained. On Windows

4789	// and releases the console (or until Close), so EOF follows Wait/Close there.
4790	// Write sends input to the terminal.
4791	// Resize changes the window size; the child is signalled with SIGWINCH on unix.
4792		// Hold the write lock across the platform call so concurrent Resizes are
4793		// serialised: the reported size always matches the last one applied to the
4794		// terminal, and Size() can never observe a half-updated value.
4795	// Size is the terminal size last requested.

4797	// Wait blocks until the child exits and returns its exit code.
4798	// Exited reports whether the child has already exited.
4799	// ExitCode is the child's exit code, or -1 while it runs.
4800	// PID is the child's process id.
4801	// TestFitSizeResultIsAlwaysResizable checks the core invariant: no matter what
4802	// garbage the resize event carries, the result satisfies Resize's >= minAxis
4803	// requirement and fits in uint16.
4804		// minAxis matches the minimum dimension Start and Resize already accept.
4805		// maxAxis is the largest value a Size axis (uint16) can hold.
4806	// FitSize constrains requested terminal dimensions into a usable range.
4807	// cols and rows are the raw dimensions of a UI/window resize event. They are
4808	// ints because such values are commonly derived from pixel measurements or
4809	// decoded from JSON, and can be zero, negative or absurdly large. maxCols and
4810	// maxRows bound the result to what the caller can accommodate; a non-positive
4811	// bound means "no caller limit" and is capped only by what Size can hold.
4812	// The result is always safe to pass to Resize: each axis is at least minAxis
4813	// and at most the effective bound, and never exceeds uint16. minAxis wins over
4814	// a smaller positive maximum so a degenerate bound cannot produce a useless
4815	// size. FitSize is pure and safe for concurrent use.

5044	// A string containing both styles cannot be formatted correctly. Passing it

5047	// catalog entry that mixes them is therefore a translation bug; callers can

5103	// outputLanguageTokenMaxLength bounds an untrusted preference token before it
5104	// is validated. Prose longer than this can never be a language designation.
5105	// bcp47Tag is a deliberately strict BCP-47 shape: a 2-3 letter primary
5106	// subtag, an optional 4-letter script, an optional region (2 letters or 3
5107	// digits), and up to two variants in the grammar's two forms. Both letter
5108	// cases are spelled out instead of using (?i): RE2's case-insensitive mode
5109	// folds confusables such as U+212A KELVIN SIGN into [a-z], which would let a
5110	// repository-authored token pass as a language tag.

5112	// is the closed set of designations the product itself writes; a token found
5113	// in a repository-writable file is trusted only when it is one of these
5114	// spellings (code, settings id, full English name, or native name).

5116	// and RE2's (?i) also fold non-ASCII confusables (e.g. U+212A KELVIN SIGN to
5117	// "k"), which would let a case-disguised token slip into the closed set.
5118				// Known non-ASCII native names are also accepted in their
5119				// lower-cased spelling; only the untrusted token is never
5120				// Unicode-folded.

5122	// from an untrusted, repository-writable preference file.

5124	// well-formed BCP-47 tag is accepted. A 13-character instruction written in
5125	// CJK needs no spaces and no punctuation, so a character class cannot tell it
5126	// apart from a language name — the closed set can. Everything else is
5127	// rejected with ok == false and an empty lang; callers must never hand the raw
5128	// string to a prompt.
5129	// This is a pure function. It reads no configuration and changes no existing
5130	// locale behaviour in this package.
5131	// Keys returns every catalog key, sorted. Tests use it to check both locales
5132	// are complete.
5133	// IssueKind classifies a problem reported by AuditTranslations.
5134		// IssueMissingText: a locale's string is empty for a key that the catalog
5135		// still defines. This is a translation that was never provided.
5136		// IssueFormatVerbMismatch: the multiset of fmt.Sprintf conversion verbs
5137		// (e.g. %s, %d) differs between locales, so one locale will render
5138		// "%!(EXTRA ...)" or miss a substituted value.
5139		// IssuePlaceholderMismatch: the multiset of named {name} placeholders used
5140		// by Format differs between locales, so one locale leaves a placeholder
5141		// visible or ignores a value the other consumes.
5142	// Issue is one translation-completeness problem found by AuditTranslations.
5143	// All fields are exported so callers (e.g. a doctor command or a test) can
5144	// inspect and format them without reaching into package internals.
5145	// AuditTranslations checks translation completeness across locales and returns
5146	// the problems found. It is a pure function: it reads only the embedded
5147	// catalog, mutates no state (including the Missing log), and returns issues
5148	// sorted by Key, Locale, Kind and Detail. A healthy catalog returns an empty,
5149	// non-nil slice.
5150	// With no arguments every supported locale (Known) is audited. Arguments are
5151	// canonicalised via Canonical, de-duplicated and sorted; unsupported locales
5152	// are ignored because the catalog only carries text for the supported codes.
5153	// For each key, the first requested locale that is EnUS is the reference (the
5154	// source strings). Every other requested locale is compared against it for:
5155	//   - empty text where the reference has text (IssueMissingText);
5156	//   - a differing multiset of fmt.Sprintf verbs (IssueFormatVerbMismatch);
5157	//   - a differing multiset of named {name} placeholders
5158	// An empty reference is itself reported as IssueMissingText for that locale.
5159	// The key set is shared by all locales in this catalog, so a key cannot be
5160	// "extra" in one locale; the meaningful omissions are empty text and dropped
5161	// placeholders, which is what this audit detects.
5162	// normalizeAuditLocales canonicalises caller input. An empty request means
5163	// "all supported locales". Unknown locales are dropped.
5164	// referenceLocale picks the locale whose text is the source for comparisons.
5165	// English is preferred; otherwise the first (sorted) locale is used.
5166	// textFor selects a locale's string from an entry. Unknown codes fall back to
5167	// English, matching T's default-locale behaviour.
5168	// isASCIILetter reports whether c is an ASCII letter.
5169	// isFmtDirectiveByte reports whether c may appear between '%' and a
5170	// conversion verb: flags, width, precision, argument index and their padding.
5171	// formatVerbs returns a count of each fmt.Sprintf conversion verb in s. A
5172	// verb is the ASCII letter that terminates a '%' directive; flags, width,
5173	// precision and argument indexes do not change it. "%%" is a literal percent
5174	// and contributes no verb. A malformed or trailing '%' is ignored.
5175	// namedPlaceholders returns a count of each {name} placeholder in s, using
5176	// the same scanning rules as Format (escaped braces are not placeholders).
5177	// describeCounts renders a count map deterministically for Issue.Detail.
5178	// T looks up one UI string.
5179	// An unknown key is returned verbatim (so the gap is visible in the UI) and
5180	// recorded for Missing. When args are supplied the template is formatted with
5181	// fmt.Sprintf, e.g. T(locale, "error.model", "boom").
5182	// placeholderNameStart reports whether c may begin a placeholder name.
5183	// Names are deliberately ASCII-only so expansion is deterministic and cannot
5184	// depend on Unicode case folding; CJK bytes (>= 0x80) never match and are
5185	// therefore copied through untouched.
5186	// placeholderAt reports the name and the index just past a {name} placeholder
5187	// beginning at text[start], if one is present. Only a complete identifier
5188	// followed by '}' counts, so adjacent text such as {names} or {a-b} is not
5189	// mistaken for a different placeholder.
5190	// Format expands named {name} placeholders in text. The text is normally a
5191	// string obtained from the embedded catalog via T (which resolves the locale);
5192	// Format itself is pure and locale-agnostic. It complements T's positional
5193	// fmt.Sprintf formatting with reorderable, self-describing placeholders.
5194	// Expansion rules:
5195	//   - A placeholder is {name} where name matches [A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*.
5196	//     It is replaced with vars[name].
5197	//   - A placeholder whose name is absent from vars is left verbatim, so a
5198	//     missing translation variable is visible rather than silently blank.
5199	//   - {{ and }} emit a literal brace; {{name}} thus renders as {name} and is
5200	//     not expanded (escaping).
5201	//   - Only a complete {identifier} is expanded, so {names} is never confused
5202	//     with {name} and text adjacent to a placeholder is preserved.
5203	//   - Multi-byte text (CJK and others) is copied through byte-for-byte.
5204	//   - A brace that does not begin a valid placeholder (e.g. {a-b}, {9x}, an
5205	//     unclosed '{') is copied verbatim; nested braces are scanned so an inner
5206	//     valid placeholder still expands.
5207	//   - Values from vars are written directly and never re-scanned, so a value
5208	//     containing braces cannot inject further placeholders.
5209	// A nil vars map is valid: every placeholder is then left intact.
5210	// Missing returns the keys (or key@locale pairs) requested but not found since
5211	// the last ResetMissing, sorted and de-duplicated.
5212	// ResetMissing clears the missing-key log.
5213	// isExplicitEnglishLocale reports whether an environment locale value is an
5214	// explicit preference for English, including the suffixes real environments
5215	// attach to it: a region ("en-GB", "en_US"), an encoding ("en_US.UTF-8",
5216	// "C.UTF-8") and a modifier ("en_US@euro"). C and POSIX are the C locale,
5217	// which is English for UI purposes.
5218	// Detect recognises Chinese by prefix, so without this check a higher-priority
5219	// variable holding an explicit English locale looks merely "unrecognised" and
5220	// is skipped; a lower-priority Chinese LANG then silently overrides the
5221	// user's own setting. Only English is special-cased: other unsupported
5222	// languages still fall through, as before.
5223		// POSIX encoding and modifier suffixes follow the language tag and do not
5224		// change which language was requested.
5225	// Detect picks a locale from the environment.
5226	// ARGENT_LOCALE wins. Then LC_ALL, LC_MESSAGES and LANG are checked: a Chinese
5227	// tag selects ZhCN, an explicit English tag (including "C"/"POSIX" and values
5228	// with region, encoding or modifier suffixes) selects DefaultLocale and stops
5229	// the search. Anything else falls through to the next variable. env must not
5230	// be nil.
5231		// The capability must work with the real embedded activity.*_one/_many keys:
5232		// select the form, then let T/fmt.Sprintf embed the count.
5233	// Plural picks the grammatical form that matches count n for locale.
5234	// Forms are passed in the order [singular, plural]. English — and any locale
5235	// that Normalize falls back to — uses forms[0] for exactly one item and
5236	// forms[1] for every other count (zero, negatives and plural alike). Chinese
5237	// has no plural distinction, so forms[0] is returned for every count; callers
5238	// normally pass a single neutral form for that locale.
5239	// With no forms Plural returns "". A missing plural form falls back to
5240	// forms[0], so a caller that provides only one form always gets that form.
5241	// Forms beyond the first two are ignored.
5242	// Selection is pure: it does no catalog lookup, records nothing in Missing and
5243	// does not format the forms themselves. A caller that wants the count embedded
5244	// typically follows with fmt.Sprintf, e.g.
5245	//	form := Plural(locale, n,
5246	//	    T(locale, "activity.command_one"),
5247	//	    T(locale, "activity.command_many"))
5248	//	line := fmt.Sprintf(form, n)
5249	// Package tokenize holds the token-cost estimate shared by the agent's budget
5250	// loop and the tools' output-volume hints. It sits below both packages so the
5251	// wide-rune table has exactly one definition: it used to be copied three times
5252	// (agent, bash volume hint, repo map) with comments asking humans to keep the
5253	// copies in sync.
5254	// The estimate is deliberately cheap and deliberately an over-estimate: it is
5255	// used to stop a prompt from overflowing a model context window, not to price
5256	// a request. Wide (CJK/fullwidth/emoji) runes cost roughly one token each in
5257	// every provider tokenizer; other runes follow the chars/4 rule. Counting them
5258	// separately avoids the severe under-estimate that chars/4 alone gives CJK,
5259	// while still over-estimating relative to the most efficient tokenizers (the
5260	// safe direction for a budget).
5261	// CharsPerToken is the rule-of-thumb ratio for English text and code.
5262	// Estimate returns the estimated token cost of text. Empty text costs nothing.
5263		// Fast path: pure-ASCII text needs neither rune decoding nor the wide-rune
5264		// table, and this estimate runs over whole transcripts several times per
5265	// estimateMixed is the rune-aware path for text that contains at least one
5266	// non-ASCII byte.
5267	// IsWide reports whether r belongs to a script that tokenizers treat as
5268	// roughly one token per rune: Han, Kana, Hangul, fullwidth forms, CJK
5269	// punctuation and common emoji.
5270	// Package archguard encodes the dependency rules from the repository's
5271	// AGENTS.md as an automated test, so architectural drift is caught by
5272	// `go test ./internal/archguard` instead of by code review alone.
5273	// The test is deliberately stdlib-only (go/parser + AST walking), adds no new
5274	// module, and never compiles the packages it inspects: it parses imports only.
5275	// It therefore works on any host, including cross-compilation targets.
5276	// allowedExternalModule is the single third-party module AGENTS.md permits,
5277	// and only inside native/ (the desktop shell). Everything under internal/
5278	// must be stdlib-only. Sub-packages of this module (e.g. mygo/ui) are allowed
5279	// because they share the same module prefix.
5280	// Allow-list rationale:
5281	//   - stdlib imports (no "." in the first path segment) are allowed everywhere;
5282	//   - imports of this repository's own module (<module>/...) are NOT third
5283	//     party and are allowed under internal/ as intra-repo references;
5284	//   - the only external exception is mygo, and only under native/;
5285	//   - the archguard test itself imports stdlib only;
5286	//   - all .go files are checked, including _test.go files, build-tag variants,
5287	//     and generated/mock sources -- there are no silent exceptions.
5288	// internalLayerRules encodes package-scoped layering rules (AGENTS.md):
5289	//   - tools never imports agent;
5290	//   - agent is a library layer and never imports the app wirer or tools
5291	//     (it depends on the ToolRunner interface, not the tools package);
5292	//   - llm is the wire layer and must stay below agent/tools/app.
5293	// Imports are matched against "<module path> + forbiddenImport".
5294				// Rule (a): internal packages are stdlib + own-module only.
5295				// Rule (b): no cgo anywhere. ("C" has no dot, so rule (a) ignores it.)
5296				// Rules (c), (d), (e): package layering.
5297		// native/ is the single external exception. Enforce that its external
5298		// imports stay within the one allow-listed module, and that cgo is absent.
5299	// goFiles returns every .go file under dir, recursively, without filtering by
5300	// build tags or file name (strict: _test.go and platform variants are included).
5301	// importPaths returns the unquoted import paths declared by file. Quotes are
5302	// stripped with strings.Trim so no extra package (strconv) is needed.
5303	// isExternal reports whether imp denotes a non-stdlib module, i.e. its first
5304	// path segment contains a dot (github.com/..., golang.org/..., gopkg.in/...).
5305	// findRepoRoot walks up from the test's working directory to the go.mod file.
5306	// readModulePath discovers the module path from go.mod rather than hardcoding
5307	// it, so the guard keeps working if the module is renamed.

5328	// TestDeltaHandlerRegistrationDoesNotRaceWithReadLoop keeps onDelta safe under
5329	// -race: readLoop snapshots the callback while another goroutine swaps it for
5330	// the next streaming round. The old code let the UI thread write b.onDelta
5331	// directly while readLoop read it, which the race detector flags.
5332		// Swap handlers concurrently with delta delivery, as sendReal does on the
5333		// UI thread when a new round begins.
5334	// TestReadLoopUsesLatestDeltaHandler proves that a replacement callback takes
5335	// over immediately: a readLoop snapshot can never deliver a delta to a handler
5336	// that a newer round has already superseded.
5337	// 1 is aborted and round 2 starts before round 1's result returns, round 1's
5338	// late completion must not run finish() (which writes app.reply, by then owned
5339	// by round 2). The generation token makes that stale completion a no-op.
5340		// Round 2 supersedes round 1 before round 1's result arrives.
5341		// Round 2's own completion still applies normally.
5342	// bridge drives the real argentgo engine over its stdio JSON-RPC transport.
5343	// The native UI stays dependency-free of the engine: it spawns `argent --rpc`
5344	// and speaks the same newline-delimited JSON-RPC that --rpc already exposes.
5345	// This keeps argentgo's core stdlib-only (no MyGo dependency baked in).
5346		// deltaGen is bumped on every onDelta registration. It stamps one
5347		// streaming round so a superseded round's late result can be dropped.
5348		// Guarded by mu together with onDelta.
5349	// startBridge launches `argent --rpc`. exePath is the path to argent.exe;
5350	// workspace is the working directory for the engine. onDelta receives streamed
5351	// prompt chunks; onEvent receives real agent event notifications (kind + the
5352	// raw agent.Event JSON) once SubscribeEvents has registered the subscription.
5353		// stderr is inherited so engine errors are visible during development.
5354	// setDeltaHandler installs fn as the current prompt/delta callback and returns
5355	// the generation it was installed at. Registration and readLoop's delivery
5356	// snapshot are serialized by mu, so swapping callbacks between streaming rounds
5357	// cannot race with (or tear) a delta being delivered. The returned generation
5358	// lets a caller retire a superseded round before its late result is applied.
5359	// isCurrentDeltaGen reports whether gen still identifies the active streaming
5360	// round. When it returns false a newer round has superseded gen, so any delta
5361	// or completion stamped with gen must be discarded.
5362	// readLoop demultiplexes responses, prompt/delta notifications and real
5363	// agent "event" notifications. All callbacks run on this single reader
5364	// goroutine; consumers must marshal UI changes onto their own thread.
5365							// Snapshot the callback under the lock so a concurrent
5366							// registration on the UI thread cannot race with this
5367							// read. Call it after unlocking: a handler may itself
5368							// call back into the bridge (e.g. isCurrentDeltaGen).
5369						// event/subscribe forwards each agent event as
5370						// {"method":"event","params":{"kind":...,"event":...}}.
5371						// Keep the raw event so the UI can decode only the fields
5372						// it renders without the bridge importing internal/agent.
5373	// call sends a request and waits for its response.
5374	// SubscribeEvents registers the connection with the engine's event hub. The
5375	// call result reports the hub's current seq; from then on every agent event is
5376	// delivered as an "event" notification which readLoop dispatches to onEvent.
5377	// ctx bounds the registration call so a stalled engine cannot block startup.
5378	// The subscription itself lives for the lifetime of the RPC transport (EOF or
5379	// bridge close tears it down), so there is no matching unsubscribe method.
5380	// prompt sends a real prompt; delta chunks arrive through onDelta.

5622	// TestRetryReplaysPromptAfterUndo checks that retry restores the exact prompt
5623	// the user typed and rebuilds a complete three-row turn.
5624	// TestRetryNoopWhenStreamingOrEmpty guards the two no-op edges of retry.
5625	// TestUndoWithNoUserMessageIsNoop checks the boundary when only engine events
5626	// (tool cards) exist: there is no turn to remove.
5627	// TestCompleteRealReplyRoutesEngineErrorThroughRedaction verifies the actual
5628	// sendReal completion path (not just the helper) surfaces a provider error as a
5629	// redacted, single-line, rune-bounded notice with an explicit status.
5630	// Only the leading `gui` subcommand may divert the CLI into the native
5631	// frontend. Scanning every argument hijacks prompts and flag values.
5632	// isGUIRequest reports whether the arguments name the native GUI subcommand.
5633	// Only a leading `gui` counts. Scanning every argument would hijack ordinary
5634	// prompts such as `argent fix gui bug`, and would also launch the GUI when a
5635	// flag consumed `gui` as its value (`argent --model gui hello`). The --gui
5636	// flag is parsed by the CLI itself, which reports it as not migrated.
5637		// main dispatches here on the leading `gui` subcommand; strip only that
5638		// token. Filtering every `gui`/`--gui` argument would swallow prompt words
5639		// and silently ignore flags the GUI parser should reject.
5640		// The session store is still opened so the native client's engine has a
5641		// ready place to persist conversations before it is launched.
5642	// launchNativeFrontend starts the MyGo native client (argent-native.exe),
5643	// which connects back to this engine over --rpc. On Windows the native binary
5644	// ships beside argent.exe in the same release zip.
5645	// Command argent is the terminal entry point.
5646		// Only the leading `gui` subcommand launches the native client. --gui is a
5647		// regular CLI flag and is handled (reported as not migrated) by cli.RunWith.
